(T^{\lambda}_{j,k}\tilde{h}_{j,k})(\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime},y):=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\R^{n-1}}T_{j,k}(\lambda,\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}-\boldsymbol{\tilde{x}}^{\prime},y+\tilde{y})\tilde{h}_{j,k}(\boldsymbol{\tilde{x}}^{\prime},\tilde{y})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\tilde{x}}^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{y},

where we recall that
T_{j,k}(\lambda,\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime},y)=\big(\frac{\partial}{\partial y}K_{j,k}^{\lambda}-K_{j,k}^{\lambda}L_{\lambda}^{1/2m}\big)(\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime},y)
.
It remains to show that
u
lies in the class
\mathrm{W}_{p,q}^{(1,2m)}(\R_{+}\times\R_{+}^{n};E)
provided
h_{j,k}=L^{\frac{2m-k}{2m}}\mathrm{e}^{-yL^{1/2m}}g_{j,k}\in\mathring{F}_{p,q}^{\frac{1}{2m}(1-\frac{1}{q})}(\R_{+};\mathrm{L}_{q}(\R_{+}^{n};E))\cap\mathrm{L}_{p}(\R_{+};\mathrm{B}_{q,q}^{1-\frac{1}{q}}(\R^{n};E))
.
By
\mathrm{L}_{p}
-maximal regularity of the operator
\partial_{y}+L^{1/2m}
, it suffices to prove that the map given by
\big((t,(\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime},y))\mapsto L^{-k/2m}\mathrm{e}^{-yL^{1/2m}}g(t,\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime})\big)
lies in
\mathrm{L}_{p,q}(\R_{+}\times\R_{+}^{n})
, if

g\in\mathring{F}_{p,q}^{\frac{1}{2m}(1-\frac{1}{q})}(\R_{+};\mathrm{L}_{q}(\R^{n-1};E))\cap\mathrm{L}_{p}(\R_{+};\mathrm{B}_{q,q}^{1-\frac{1}{q}}(\R^{n-1};E)).

To show this property, we employ
[
18
, Lemma 6.2]
which shows that for such
g
, the functions defined by
\big((t,(\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime},y))\mapsto B\mathrm{e}^{-yB}g(t,\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime})\big)
and
\big((t,(\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime},y))\mapsto C\mathrm{e}^{-yC}g(t,\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime})\big)
belong to the space
\mathrm{L}_{p,q}(\R_{+}\times\R_{+}^{n+1};E)
, where
B=(\partial_{t})^{1/2m}
denotes the fractional time derivative with domain
\dom(B)=\mathrm{W}_{p}^{\frac{1}{2m}}(\R_{+};\mathrm{L}_{q}(\R^{n-1};E))
and
C=(-\Delta)^{1/2}
denote the square of (minus) the (spatial) Laplace operator with domain
\dom(C)=\mathrm{L}_{p}(\R_{+};\mathrm{B}_{q,q}^{1}(\R^{n-1};E))
.
These operators commute on
\dom(L^{1/2m})=\dom(B)\cap\dom(C)
.
Moreover, since
B+C+1
is relatively bounded w.r.t.
L^{1/2m}
, by the perturbation theorem for
\mathcal{R}
-sectorial operators Proposition
2.11
, for sufficiently small
\eta>0
, the perturbed operator
L^{1/2m}-\eta(B+C+1)
generates a bounded holomorphic semigroup on
\mathrm{L}_{p,q}(\R_{+}\times\R^{n-1};E)
.
Also, since the operators
\partial_{y}
(with
\dom(\partial_{y})=\{u=u(t,(\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime},y))\in\mathrm{L}_{p,q}(\R_{+}\times\R_{+}^{n};E):\,\partial_{y}u\in\mathrm{L}_{p,q}(\R_{+}\times\R_{+}^{n};E)\}
) and
L^{1/2m}
are resolvent-commutative, by the Dore–Venni Theorem, cf.
[
17
, Corollary 1.6]
, the abstract Cauchy problem

\begin{cases}\partial_{y}w+L^{1/2m}w=f,&y>0,\\
w(0,\cdot)=0\end{cases}

has
\mathrm{L}_{q}
-maximal regularity as well.
(Note that for the function
w
we switched the order of the variables to
(y,t,\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime})
.)
Thus, for
f(y,(t,\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime})):=(B+C+1)\mathrm{e}^{-\eta(B+C+1)y}g(t,\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime})
we obtain a solution
w=w(y,(t,\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}))\in\mathrm{W}_{q}^{1}(\R_{+};\mathrm{L}_{p,q}(\R_{+}\times\R^{n-1};E))\cap\mathrm{L}_{q}(\R_{+};\mathrm{W}_{p,q}^{(1,2)}(\R_{+}\times\R^{n-1};E))
for which, in particular, the Duhamel formula is valid:

w(y,\cdot)=\int_{0}^{y}\mathrm{e}^{-L^{1/2m}(y-\tilde{y})}(B+C+1)\mathrm{e}^{-\eta(B+C+1)\tilde{y}}g\mathrm{d}\tilde{y}.

To employ integration by parts in
y
, observe that

\displaystyle(B+C+1)\mathrm{e}^{-\eta(B+C+1)\tilde{y}}g

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{-\eta(C+1)\tilde{y}}B\mathrm{e}^{-\eta B\tilde{y}}g+\mathrm{e}^{-\eta(B+1)\tilde{y}}C\mathrm{e}^{-\eta C\tilde{y}}g+\mathrm{e}^{-(B+C+1)\tilde{y}}g

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{-\eta(C+1)\tilde{y}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{y}}\mathrm{e}^{-\eta B\tilde{y}}g+\mathrm{e}^{-\eta(B+1)\tilde{y}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{y}}\mathrm{e}^{-\eta C\tilde{y}}g+\mathrm{e}^{-(B+C+1)\tilde{y}}g,

and note that
B
and
C
commute on
\dom(B)\cap\dom(C)
,
(\mathrm{e}^{-\eta(B+\frac{1}{2})\tilde{y}})_{\tilde{y}\geq 0}
and
(\mathrm{e}^{-\eta(C+\frac{1}{2})\tilde{y}})_{\tilde{y}\geq 0}
are bounded semigroups on
\mathrm{L}_{p,q}(\R_{+}\times\R^{n-1};E)
.
Therefore, each summand in the above representation of the function
(B+C+1)\mathrm{e}^{\eta(B+C+1)\tilde{y}}g
lies in
\mathrm{L}_{q}(\R_{+};\mathrm{L}_{p,q}(\R_{+}\times\R^{n-1};E))\cap\mathrm{C}(\R_{+};\mathrm{L}_{p,q}(\R_{+}\times\R^{n-1};E)
and, therefore, is a measurable function on
\R\times\R_{+}
with values in
\mathrm{L}_{q}(\R^{n-1};E)
.
Invoking the vector-valued version of Fubini’s Theorem
[
4
, Theorem 6.16]
and the vector-valued characterisation of the spaces
\mathrm{L}_{q}(\R;\mathrm{L}_{q}(\R^{n-1};E))
and
\mathrm{L}_{q}(\R^{n};E)
, see
[
4
, Bemerkung 6.19]
, we may interpret this as an element of
\mathrm{L}_{p,q}(\R_{+}\times\R^{n};E)
and via integration by parts we may conclude that

\displaystyle w(y,\cdot)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\eta}\int_{0}^{y}L^{1/2m}\mathrm{e}^{-L^{1/2m}(y-\tilde{y})}\mathrm{e}^{-\eta(B+C+1)\tilde{y}}g\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{y}-\frac{1}{\eta}\big[\mathrm{e}^{-L^{1/2m}(y-\tilde{y})}\mathrm{e}^{-\eta(B+C+1)\tilde{y}}g\big]_{\tilde{y}=0}^{\tilde{y}=y}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\eta}L^{1/2m}(B+C+1)^{-1}w(y,\cdot)+\frac{1}{\eta}\big(\mathrm{e}^{-L^{1/2m}y}-\mathrm{e}^{-\eta(B+C+1)y}\big)g,

and, therefore, after rearranging the terms and multiplying by
L^{1/2m}
from the left,

\displaystyle L^{1/2m}\mathrm{e}^{-L^{1/2m}y}g

\displaystyle=L^{1/2m}\mathrm{e}^{-\eta(B+C+1)y}g+L^{1/2m}\big(\eta-L^{1/2m}(B+C+1)^{-1})w(y,\cdot)

\displaystyle=L^{1/2m}\mathrm{e}^{-\eta(B+C+1)y}g+\big(\eta-L^{1/2m}(B+C+1)^{-1})L^{1/2m}w(y,\cdot),

also employing that
L_{\lambda}
commutes with
B
and
C
.
Here, on the right-hand side
\mathrm{e}^{-\eta(B+C+1)y}g
and
w\in\mathrm{W}_{p,q}(\R_{+}\times\R_{+}^{n+1};E)
,
L^{1/2m}
maps
\mathrm{L}_{q}(\R_{+};\mathrm{W}_{p,q}(\R_{+}\times\R^{n-1};E))
into
\mathrm{L}_{q}(\R_{+};\mathrm{L}_{p,q}(\R_{+}\times\R^{n-1};E))
and
L^{1/2m}(B+C+1)^{-1}
is a bounded linear operator on
\mathrm{L}_{q}(\R_{+};\mathrm{L}_{p,q}(\R_{+}\times\R^{n-1};E))
(which is isometrically isomorphic to
\mathrm{L}_{p,q}(\R_{+}\times\R_{+}^{n+1};E)
via
\tilde{w}(t,(\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime},y)):=w(y,(t,\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}))
, hence, we obtain that
\big((t,(\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime},y))\mapsto L^{1/2m}\mathrm{e}^{-L^{1/2m}y}g(t,\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime})\big)\in\mathrm{L}_{p,q}(\R_{+}\times\R_{+}^{n};E)
as claimed.
∎

This also finishes the proof of Theorem
6.2
, up to perturbation and localisation.
We leave the latter, rather technical steps to the interested reader.

7.
Appendix

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
\lambda>0
,
E
be any Banach space and
p\in(1,\infty)
.
Then, the operator
-A_{\lambda}
defined as the
\mathrm{L}_{p}(\R^{n};E)
-realisation of the Fourier symbol
-(\lambda+\left|\boldsymbol{\xi}\right|^{2m})^{1/2m}
generates a bounded
\mathrm{C}_{0}
-semigroup on
\mathrm{L}_{p}(\R^{n};E)
.

Proof.

In view of the Hille-Yosida Theorem for semigroups of class
(M,\omega)=(M,0)

[
24
, Theorem II.3.8]
, we need to show that
\mu+A_{\lambda}
is continuously invertible on
\mathrm{L}_{p}(\R^{n};E)
for every
\mu>0
and there is a constant

(30)

\left\|(\mu(\mu+A_{\lambda})^{-1})^{k}\right\|_{\B(\mathrm{L}_{p}(\R^{n};E))}\leq C

for some constant
C>0
independent of
\mu>0
and
k\in\N
.
To this end, we note that the Fourier symbol of
\mu+A_{\lambda}
is
\mu+(\lambda+\left|\boldsymbol{\xi}\right|^{2m})^{1/2m}
, hence, it suffices to show that
\frac{\mu}{\mu+(\lambda+\left|\boldsymbol{\xi}\right|^{2m})^{1/2m}}
is a Fourier-multiplier on
\mathrm{L}_{p}(\R^{n};E)
and the norm estimates (
30
) are valid.
In view of the Mikhlin multiplier theorem, we will show that

(31)

\left|\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\partial_{\boldsymbol{\xi}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\big(\frac{\mu^{k}}{(\mu+(\lambda+\left|\boldsymbol{\xi}\right|^{2m})^{1/2m})^{k}}\big)\right|\leq C_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\quad\text{for }\left|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\right|\leq\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor+1,\,k\in\N,\,\boldsymbol{\xi}\in\R^{n},\,\mu>0,

for constants
C_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}>0
independent of
k\in\N
and
\mu>0
.
In that case
(0,\infty)\subseteq\rho(-A_{\lambda})
and (
30
) is valid.
Estimate (
31
) will follow by combining Lemma
7.2
and Lemma
7.3
below with the fact that any polynomial
p
of order
k
can be estimated by
c_{n}^{p}\binom{n+k}{k}
for any
n\in\N
and a suitable constant
c_{n}^{p}>0
.
Since the operator symbol is scalar, the classical, scalar version of the Mikhlin multiplier theorem can be invoked to show that
\left\|(\mu(\mu+A_{\lambda})^{-1})^{k}\right\|_{\B(\mathrm{L}_{p}(\R^{n})}\leq C
for all
\mu>0
and
k\in\N_{0}
, as the operator norm does not depend on the choice of the Banach space
E\neq\{0\}
, i.e. it coincides with the operator norm for the corresponding scalar-valued operator, where
E
is replaced by
\K
.
Consequently,
-A_{\lambda}
generates a bounded
\mathrm{C}_{0}
-semigroup on
\mathrm{L}_{p}(\R^{n};E)
.
∎

Lemma 7.2
.

For every
N\in\N
,
\mathcal{I}=(i_{1},\ldots,i_{N})\in\{1,\ldots,n\}^{N}
, there are
J_{\mathcal{I}}\in\N
, polynomials
p_{\mathcal{I},j}
of order
\leq N
, polynomials
q_{\mathcal{I},j}
of order
\leq N-1
and exponents
\alpha_{\mathcal{I},j}^{(l)}\in\N_{0}
,
l=0,1,\ldots,N
,
\beta_{\mathcal{I},j}\in\N_{0}
with

\beta_{\mathcal{I},j}=\sum_{l=0}^{n}\alpha_{\mathcal{I},j}^{(l)}+\operatorname{deg}(q_{\mathcal{I},j})\quad\text{and}\quad\beta_{\mathcal{I},j}\leq 2mN

such that

\displaystyle\big(\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi_{i_{1}}}\cdots\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi_{i_{N}}}\big)\frac{\mu^{k}}{(\mu+(\lambda+\left|\boldsymbol{\xi}\right|^{2m})^{1/2m})^{k}}

(32)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu^{k}}{(\mu+(\lambda+\left|\boldsymbol{\xi}\right|^{2m})^{1/2m})^{k+n}}\sum_{j=1}^{J_{\mathcal{I}}}p_{\mathcal{I},j}(k)q_{\mathcal{I},j}(\mu)\cdot\prod_{l=1}^{d}\xi_{l}^{\alpha_{\mathcal{I},j}^{(l)}}\cdot\left|\boldsymbol{\xi}\right|^{\alpha_{\mathcal{I},j}^{(0)}}\cdot(\lambda+\left|\boldsymbol{\xi}\right|^{2m})^{-\frac{\beta_{\mathcal{I},j}}{2m}}.

Proof.

We proceed by induction over
N\in\N
.
For
N=1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi_{i_{1}}}\big(\frac{\mu^{k}}{(\mu+(\lambda+\left|\boldsymbol{\xi}\right|^{2m})^{1/2m})^{k}}\big)=\frac{\mu^{k}}{(\mu+(\lambda+\left|\boldsymbol{\xi}\right|^{2m})^{1/2m})^{k}}\big(-k\xi_{i_{1}}\left|\boldsymbol{\xi}\right|^{2m-1}(\lambda+\left|\boldsymbol{\xi}\right|^{2m})^{-\frac{2m-1}{2m}}\big),

so that we may choose
\mathcal{I}=\{i_{1}\}
,
J_{\mathcal{I}}=1
,
p_{\mathcal{I},1}(k)=k
,
q_{\mathcal{I},1}(\mu)=1
,
\alpha_{\mathcal{I},1}^{(l)}=\delta_{l,i_{1}}
for
l=1,\ldots,N
,
\alpha_{\mathcal{I},1}^{(0)}=2m-1
and
\beta_{\mathcal{I},1}=2m-1
.

Now, provided the claim is valid for
\mathcal{I}=(i_{1},\ldots,i_{N})\in\{1,\ldots,n\}^{N}
, it follows for
(i_{1},\ldots,i_{N},i_{N+1})
that

\displaystyle\big(\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi_{i_{1}}}\cdots\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi_{i_{N+1}}}\big)\frac{\mu^{k}}{(\mu+(\lambda+\left|\boldsymbol{\xi}\right|^{2m})^{1/2m})^{k}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi_{i_{N+1}}}\big(\frac{\mu^{k}}{(\mu+(\lambda+\left|\boldsymbol{\xi}\right|^{2m})^{1/2m})^{k+n}}\sum_{j=1}^{J_{\mathcal{I}}}p_{\mathcal{I},j}(k)q_{\mathcal{I},j}(\mu)\cdot\prod_{l=1}^{d}\xi_{l}^{\alpha_{\mathcal{I},j}^{(l)}}\cdot\left|\boldsymbol{\xi}\right|^{\alpha_{\mathcal{I},j}^{(0)}}\cdot(\lambda+\left|\boldsymbol{\xi}\right|^{2m})^{-\frac{\beta_{\mathcal{I},j}}{2m}}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu^{k}}{(\mu+(\lambda+\left|\boldsymbol{\xi}\right|^{2m})^{1/2m})^{k+n+1}}(-(k+N))\xi_{i_{N+1}}\left|\boldsymbol{\xi}\right|^{2m-1}(\lambda+\left|\boldsymbol{\xi}\right|^{2m})^{-\frac{2m-1}{2m}}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{J_{\mathcal{I}}}p_{\mathcal{I},j}(k)q_{\mathcal{I},j}(\mu)\cdot\prod_{l=1}^{d}\xi_{l}^{\alpha_{\mathcal{I},j}^{(l)}}\cdot\left|\boldsymbol{\xi}\right|^{\alpha_{\mathcal{I},j}^{(0)}}\cdot(\lambda+\left|\boldsymbol{\xi}\right|^{2m})^{-\frac{\beta_{\mathcal{I},j}}{2m}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\mu^{k}}{(\mu+(\lambda+\left|\boldsymbol{\xi}\right|^{2m})^{1/2m})^{k+n}}\sum_{j=1}^{J_{\mathcal{I}}}p_{\mathcal{I},j}(k)q_{\mathcal{I},j}(\mu)\cdot\big(\alpha_{\mathcal{I},j}^{(i_{N+1})}\xi_{i_{N+1}}^{\alpha_{\mathcal{I},j}^{(i_{N+1})}-1}\prod_{l\neq i_{N+1}}\xi_{l}^{\alpha_{\mathcal{I},j}^{(l)}}\cdot

\displaystyle\qquad\left|\boldsymbol{\xi}\right|^{\alpha_{\mathcal{I},j}^{(0)}}\cdot(\lambda+\left|\boldsymbol{\xi}\right|^{2m})^{-\frac{\beta_{\mathcal{I},j}}{2m}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\alpha_{\mathcal{I},j}^{(0)}\prod_{l=1}^{n}\xi_{l}^{\alpha_{\mathcal{I},j}^{(l)}}\cdot\xi_{i_{N+1}}\left|\boldsymbol{\xi}\right|^{\alpha_{\mathcal{I},j}^{(0)}-2}\cdot(\lambda+\left|\boldsymbol{\xi}\right|^{2m})^{-\frac{\beta_{\mathcal{I},j}}{2m}}

\displaystyle\qquad-\beta_{\mathcal{I},j}\xi_{i_{N+1}}\left|\boldsymbol{\xi}\right|^{2m-2}\prod_{l=1}^{n}\xi_{l}^{\alpha_{\mathcal{I},j}^{(l)}}\cdot\left|\boldsymbol{\xi}\right|^{\alpha_{\mathcal{I},j}^{(0)}}\cdot(\lambda+\left|\boldsymbol{\xi}\right|^{-\frac{\beta_{\mathcal{I},j}+2m}{2m}}\big)

and rearranging the term, this shows that this partial derivative of order
N+1
has the form (
32
).
Now the claim follows by induction over
N
.
∎

Lemma 7.3
.

For all
a,b\in[0,\infty)
and
M,N\in\N
:

a^{M}b^{N}\leq\frac{M!\cdot N!}{(M+N)!}(a+b)^{M+N}.

Proof.

It holds that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{k}}{\partial a^{k}}a^{M}b^{N}

\displaystyle=\frac{M!}{(M-k+1)!}a^{M-k}b^{N},

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{k}}{\partial a^{k}}(a+b)^{M+N}

\displaystyle=\frac{(M+N)!}{(M+N-k+1)!}(a+b)^{M+N-k},\quad k=1,\ldots,M.

Moreover, for
a=0
,

\left.\frac{\partial^{k}}{\partial a^{k}}a^{M}b^{N}\right|_{a=0}=0\quad\text{for }k=0,1,\ldots,M-1,

whereas

\left.\frac{\partial^{k}}{\partial a^{k}}(a+b)^{M+N}\right|_{a=0}\geq 0\quad\text{for }k=0,1,\ldots,M-1.

Therefore,

\displaystyle a^{M}b^{N}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{a}\int_{0}^{s_{1}}\cdots\int_{0}^{s_{M-1}}\frac{\partial^{M}}{\partial s_{M}^{M}}(s_{M}^{M}b^{N})\,\mathrm{d}s_{M_{1}}\cdots\mathrm{d}s_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{a}\int_{0}^{s_{1}}\cdots\int_{0}^{s_{M-1}}M!\cdot b^{N}\,\mathrm{d}s_{M_{1}}\cdots\mathrm{d}s_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{a}\int_{0}^{s_{1}}\cdots\int_{0}^{s_{M-1}}M!\cdot(s_{M}+b)^{N}\,\mathrm{d}s_{M_{1}}\cdots\mathrm{d}s_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{a}\int_{0}^{s_{1}}\cdots\int_{0}^{s_{M-1}}\frac{M!\cdot N!}{(M+N)!}\cdot\frac{\partial^{M}}{\partial s_{M}^{M}}(s_{M}+b)^{M+N}\,\mathrm{d}s_{M_{1}}\cdots\mathrm{d}s_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M!\cdot N!}{(M+N)!}(a+b)^{M+N}.

∎
